#6923. The 25 Sexiest Movies on Prime Video Right Now

Including the best erotic thrillers, raunchy comedies, and all the mess in between.

Even in just the past decade, sexy cinema has come a long way from Fifty Shades of Grey. No judgement here—the entire trilogy is available to stream on Prime Video if you haven’t seen the Dakota Johnson and Jamie Dornan-starring franchise. But if you’re looking to expand your range a bit, we have a lot to recommend.

Sexy movies are having a comeback in 2026, thanks to Wuthering Heights, I Want Your Sex, and—another Olivia Wilde film—The Invite. While Emerald Fennell adapted Emily Bronte’s novel into a tortured and depraved sexual experience, Wilde’s I Want Your Sex and The Invite used sex more as a vehicle for dark comedy. But it’s not just new films on this list. We can also point you to Weekend, Ghost, Before Sunrise, and just any kind of sexy movie you’re looking for on screen.

Below, you’ll find our pick of the 25 sexiest movies you can stream on Prime Video right now.

Olivia Wilde and Cooper Hoffman star in Gregg Araki’s raunchy workplace romantic comedy about a high profile artist (Wilde) who ropes one of her new assistants (Hoffman) into a dominant/submissive relationship. At first, he enjoys the attention, but the rollercoaster of their sexual power dynamic eventually drives them both insane.

Sydney Sweeney does Rear Window meets Peeping Tom in this sexy crime drama about a couple who becomes obsessed with the sex lives of the people they can see from their apartment window across the street. The film also stars Justice Smith and Ahsoka’s Natasha Liu Bordizzo.

No Hard Feelings follows a down-on-her-luck Uber driver (Jennifer Lawrence) who is hired by a very wealthy couple to take their inexperienced nineteen-year-old son (Andrew Barth Feldman) on a date to boost his confidence before he leaves for college. The film treads dangerous waters, but Lawrence won over the audience and earned herself a Golden Globe nomination for the sexy comedy.

Love her or hate her, Emerald Fennell can make a steamy movie. Wuthering Heights is a little more of a reimagining than an adaptation, taking quite a few liberties with the text, but it puts the romantic and sexual dynamics right at the forefront of the story. And there’s no beating Margot Robbie and Jacob Elordi as the leads.

The Fault in Our Stars director Josh Boone teams up with It Ends With Us novelist Colleen Hoover for a romance drama about a teen pregnancy that alters the course of two lovers’ lives. That is, until later in life, when the couple (Allison Wiliams and Dave Franco) reunite to explore what could have been.

Emerald Fennell’s steamy The Talented Mr. Ripley–esque thriller, Saltburn, follows a young student at Oxford University who becomes infatuated (read: dangerously obsessed) with one of his aristocratic classmates, who invites him to his family’s grand estate. It’s a darkly funny and wildly intense ride.

The phenom that saved dark romance novels: Fifty Shades of Grey and the entire Fifty Shades trilogy is available to stream on Prime Video. The story, for those who still don’t know, follows a recent college graduate (Dakota Johnson) who signs a contract to take part in a sexual bondage relationship with billionaire Christian Gray (Jamie Dornan).

L’inconnu du lac (known as Stranger by the Lake) is one of the most acclaimed erotic thrillers of the 21st century, picking up Best Director and the Queer Palm awards in the year it premiered at the Cannes Film Festival. In Stranger by the Lake, Franck, a man in his 30s, meets Michel by a lake where gay men often bathe and immediately falls for him. What he doesn’t know, however, is that Michel is a psychopathic murderer. Stranger by the Lake is as smart as it is sexy, and it will truly keep you on your toes.

Who said rom-coms can’t be sexy too? Secretary charts the dominant-submissive relationship that blossoms between a lawyer and his secretary. It’s certainly on the crazier side and may not be for everyone, but Secretary is a brilliantly acted film that feels quite far ahead of its time for such a mainstream movie in its original take on a romantic BDSM relationship.

Carol does something a little different: It’s a slow-burning romance with sensuality bubbling beneath the surface. It follows a department-store worker and aspiring photographer whose life takes an unexpected turn when she falls in love with a mysterious, beautiful older woman going through a difficult divorce. Todd Haynes’s Oscar-nominated period romance is a masterpiece of modern queer cinema, with career-defining performances from Rooney Mara and Cate Blanchett. Hugely romantic and remarkably executed, Carol is a special film that’ll certainly stick with you.

Alongside Four Weddings and a Funeral, Sirens was one of the films that brought generational British heartthrob Hugh Grant to American audiences. Grant plays a progressive reverend who, with his wife, pays a visit to an eccentric artist whose work is viewed as blasphemous. His wife soon finds herself drawn to the three models working with the painter, and what ensues is a clever breakdown of 1930s sexual repression.

Everyone loves a meet-cute, and Weekend’s is as brilliant as they come. Russell thinks he’s signed up for a one-night stand with Glen, a man he meets in a club, but over the course of a single weekend, a deeper relationship quickly grows between the two. Touching, oddly realistic, and distinctly British in its sense of humor, Weekend is sexy, funny, and grounded in a way that few romantic comedy-dramas manage.

In Eternity, Elizabeth Olsen plays a woman who, upon reaching the afterlife, is forced to choose between two people to spend eternity with: her first husband, who died young, and her second husband of 65 years. Eternity is infectiously sweet, a little sexy, and an essential modern comedy.

It might be a stretch to call this one “sexy,” per se, but you won’t find a romantic drama quite like this one—and you definitely won’t find one that’s more intense. Luca Guadagnino’s Bones and All is a road-trip drama that follows a young woman who develops feelings for a disillusioned young drifter on a 3,000-mile journey across America. Oh, and they’re both cannibals.

Wong Kar-wai’s romantic masterpiece proves that a film can be truly romantic, sensual, and intense while still holding solidly on to a PG rating. In the Mood for Love follows a man and a woman who discover that their spouses are having an affair with one another, but as they bond, they begin to develop their own connection. This is one for the yearners.

Sexy films are by no means a new phenomenon, and Caligula is one of the originals. Though the original was pretty poorly received, the film recently received a restoration and rerelease that reconstructed the film entirely from footage that didn’t make the final cut.

Love triangles don’t get much more complicated than this, and sports dramas don’t get much sexier. Challengers follows two friends who fall in love with the same young tennis prodigy and the complicated chaos that ensues when their relationships get messy. Tennis has never looked so thrilling

Set in the dreamy Italian countryside, Call Me by Your Name remains the film that introduced Timothée Chalamet to the masses as a future leading man (and got him his first Oscar nomination). Here he plays a teenager who falls in love with his father’s assistant and comes to terms with his sexuality. The film is sad, symbolic, and deeply romantic.

The legendary leading duo of Patrick Swayze and Demi Moore speaks for itself. Ghost is maybe a little more on the sentimental side than some of the other “sexy films” in Prime Video’s lineup, but it’s a deeply moving one worth your time.

Portrait of a Lady on Fire was an immediate hit with critics and audiences when it was released in 2019. The film follows an artist commissioned to paint an aristocratic heiress, but things become more complicated when the two fall in love. Romantic films don’t get more French than this one, which is also loosely based on the timeless tale of Orpheus and Eurydice.

Nicole Kidman and Harris Dickinson are an absolutely electrifying onscreen pair in this erotic thriller that sees a CEO begin an affair with her power-hungry intern, putting the stability of her family life and career on the line. Kidman picked up the Best Actress award at the Venice Film Festival for her role, and it’s been called one of her most ambitious performances to date.

Can anything capture your heart like a meet-cute romance? American traveler Jesse and French student Céline unceremoniously meet on a train from Budapest to Vienna, deciding on a whim to spend his last hours on the continent together. It’s the first in Richard Linklater’s acclaimed Before trilogy, and it sets you up for a brilliant slow burn.

If a Skarsgård signed up for it, I’m interested. He’s about to play a love interest made out of wicker, but last year Alexander Skarsgård played the mysteriously sexy biker-gang leader Ray in Pillion. The romantic black comedy tells the story of a shy man who begins a dominant-submissive relationship with the aforementioned biker, and it traces his personal growth as he navigates their dynamic. Pillion is sexy, crazy, and downright hilarious. It’s British black comedy at its finest.

Starring the king of crazy, surprisingly sexy indie movies, James Spader himself, Sex, Lies and Videotape is a true cult classic. The film revolves around a married couple with intimacy problems whose lives are changed when a mysterious drifter with a peculiar interest comes to stay with them.

David Cronenberg’s notorious erotic thriller is perhaps the most transgressive entry on this list, following a group of people who become sexually fixated on car crashes. Adapted from J.G. Ballard’s novel and starring James Spader, Holly Hunter, and Deborah Kara Unger, Crash is disturbing, provocative, and about as daring as erotic cinema gets.

#6922. Women’s Sports Teams Deserve Better Than What Their Homes Are Giving Them

Apparently, we’re going through this...again!

Gotham FC, the current reigning National Women’s Soccer Team champions, are on their way to winning another title. But their journey—which includes the chance to host their first semifinal game at home—has come with a major surprise: a change of venues. After their quarterfinal match against the Seattle Reign FC ended with a messed-up pitch and a fine against Gotham FC due to its conditions, the team was forced to find another place for their next match. The bigger issue? Their only option is a 5,000-person stadium located in Randall’s Island, New York.

Over 11,000 people made their way to Sports Illustrated Stadium—the home to Gotham FC as well as the MLS’s New York Red Bulls—to see them take down Seattle Reign FC. This means that less than half of that audience will be able to catch them at the match that can lead them to the finals.

While pitch conditions are absolutely important for the safety of the players and a big enough reason to change venues, the option to upgrade the pitch to a hybrid model was shut down by none other than New York Red Bulls due to a friendly game—AKA a game that does not count towards their season—they have on Saturday. Although the pitch would be ready for Gotham FC’s big game, RBNY shut down the plan as it wouldn’t be ready for their planned friendly. All of a sudden, a game-making match for a winning women’s team was being moved to a much smaller space due to a men’s game that does not count towards anything.

If you’re getting a sense of deja vu, that’s because this is not the first time a women’s team had to change their plans due to a men’s team. The WNBA’s Atlanta Dream were forced to move one of their WNBA Finals games from 8 p.m. to 12:30 p.m. on a Thursday. While the move was caused by them switching venues—going from their home at the Gateway Center, which fits 3,500 to the State Farm Arena which seats 17,000—it was also caused by a scheduled Atlanta Hawks game, a team that hasn’t won an NBA title since the 1950s.

It’s clear that no matter where they go, these women’s teams’ homes are not giving them what they need: a space where they can continue to host their ever-growing fanbases without fear of having to fall to the needs of a men’s team. The WNBA had a 21 percent increase in attendance to their games in 2026. The NWSL also gained some new faces in the crowd with a 3 percent increase, according to Sports Business Journal.

In 2028, Gotham FC will move to Etihad Park, a new stadium that will hold 25,000 fans in Queens, NYC. However, they’ll still have the problem of sharing the field with New York City FC, meaning this problem can always come up again.

A clear solution would be to give these incredible teams their own venues, but it’s evident that, financially, building a new stadium is not the easiest for some of these teams who are still growing. But, maybe next time, these men’s teams will just get the hint and let the real winners take over. It’s their home, too, after all.

#6916. The Unexpected Benefits of Actually Calling Someone

Thirty minutes on the old blower is better than therapy.

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

The options for communication are endless. Getting in touch with someone has never been easier, which also means we are subject to frequent digital conversations we’d rather not have. You can thank our overlords in Big Tech for that. Friends, family, enemies, and strangers alike can pepper open in-boxes with questions and concerns ranging from the unnecessary to full-scale attacks on your character, tastes, and loosely held opinions. Nearly 90 percent of Americans use text messaging or messaging apps daily, with 29 percent saying they’re texting more than they were one year ago, according to a 2026 YouGov study. Even with notifications turned off, they lurk in the background, and the social pressure to respond is hard for almost anyone to ignore.

But there is an anecdote to this scourge of modern life.

As an older millennial, I am part of the generation that remembers when the Internet changed our lives, but I also experienced defining moments using a simple telephone. Even in the infant stages of the Internet, the humble telephone, attached via cord that plugged into a wall, was the conduit for good and bad news; it was how we made plans and how relationships grew and solidified. I spent hours with the handset cradled between my teenage double chin and shoulder, plotting to see bands play, staying up too late talking to girls, and calling phone-sex hotlines to see if they were real. (They were.)

What younger generations don’t understand—to their detriment—is that the immediacy and intimacy of the phone call produce different results from a text or direct message. Imagine asking your crush to prom and getting three dots on the screen over and over, your stomach doing backflips as you wait for a yes or no. The phone call is the communication version of getting punched in the face. Your knee-jerk reactions are on display. The ear on the other end of the receiver can sense emotions: anger, reluctance, joy, excitement. You feel all of it innately without FaceTime video.

As adults, there are several instances—even today, especially today—for which a phone call is superior to a text message. One of the greatest is simply catching up with an old friend. Seriously, when’s the last time you cold-called a buddy? Thirty minutes on the blower with one of your besties can be exhilarating. You cover far more ground with less wrist pain, and you can do it while driving or sitting as a passenger princess, putting your wired earbuds to good use. Yes, the shock of seeing your name appear might cause a moment of panic in your friend—Who died? But they’ll get over it fast, and quickly you’ll fall into an easy conversation. Don’t overthink it; just call someone.

In business, the phone call is even more crucial. If you can get someone on the phone, they almost have to give you an answer. Even if it isn’t the one you want to hear, you can move on with your life and stop sending follow-up emails every 48 hours. Efficiency is king, and the phone can help. After all, there’s a reason that in nearly every photo of a captain of industry he or she is walking and talking on a phone. They didn’t get to where they are through text alone. Plus, if you’re under 30, the person on the other end might even be impressed that you made the effort to pick up the phone and call. They’ll certainly remember you!

Of course, nostalgia plays a part, but I’m not suggesting you buy an old-school phone and hook it up to the nearest wall. Let’s focus on the practice; your iPhone has a calling function. Use it more frequently. Sometimes the newest thing isn’t the best. We have yet to improve on the phone call. Don’t be scared to call someone or to answer when it rings. Pick up and be happy someone is on the other side. That’s a blessing in its own right.

#6914. Everything We Know About ‘The Body’ Season 2

Need more of The Body? The Netflix series is off to an excellent start, with fresh new characters and spooky, sexy situations that really take some turns. Here’s what we know about whether or not The Body has been renewed for season 2.

Set at a Catholic high school in the so-called innocence of 2004, the first season of The Body is about two girls who can’t express their feelings for each other, an ambitious dance team heading to regionals, and a mass hysteria that leads to prophetic visions and Satanic rituals. Totes normal, right? Once you’ve sped through season 1, you’ll be on the edge of your seat waiting to see what happens next.

As of the season 1 drop, The Body has not yet been renewed for season 2. It’s still early, however, and the buzz around the show has been good. Fingers crossed!

The Body is an original idea from Quinn Shepherd, not based on any pre-existing IP. So what happens next is entirely up to her and her team of writers and producers. Luckily, the first season ends with a cliff-hanger and a lot of unanswered questions.

“We always knew that that was where the show was going, Shepherd said in a post-mortem interview with Deadline about *that* thing with Grace. “From the beginning, that was the arc of the first season, and I think a lot of that has to do with things we would love to do next season and how to keep carrying the story.”

Executive producer Gina Gammell also mentioned Anya confessing to Father Franklin, Maddie’s prophecy, and the mystery over whether Tyler and Lisbeth actually slept together as potential avenues for season 2.

Given that the show doesn’t have a lot of movie stars with busy schedules in major roles, and filming on season 1 took place in 2025, it stands to reason that The Body could easily return in late 2027. But sometimes Netflix shows take a little longer. So again... cross your fingers and do everything short of ritual sacrifice to ensure The Body comes back for season 2 as soon as possible.

#6909. 10 Minimalist Thanksgiving Manicures That Are Perfect for the Holiday—How to Recreate

Celebrity nail artists share their favorite chic yet simple looks.

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

First and foremost, Thanksgiving is about spending time with family, and then, eating all the food you can possibly ingest in the stretchiest pair of pants you own. (The two priorities can be slightly interchangeable.) And while you’re planning your outfit for the holiday, why not have a chic manicure to match? I mean, your fingers will be the ones at center stage holding a fork all night.

If you don’t want to go all out with adorned turkeys and food-themed designs, there are plenty of Thanksgiving manicure ideas that are equal parts chic and on-theme. “Minimalist doesn’t have to mean forgettable,” says celebrity nail artist Max Estrada. “Sometimes a beautiful neutral, a little sparkle, or the perfect French tip says everything.” Below, I chatted with manicurists for their favorite nail looks for the holiday feast.

Keep scrolling minimalist Thanksgiving nail ideas, from earthy polishes and elevated designs that will look gorgeous well into the end of November.

“For those who prefer a minimalist look, I’d recommend a classic French manicure with a seasonal twist, such as chocolate brown tips on a sheer nude base,” says celebrity nail artist Vikki Swan.

“Another beautiful option is using different shades from the same color family across the nails, transitioning from lighter to deeper tones,” Swan explains. “It’s a simple yet elegant way to capture the fall mood without intricate nail art.” This easy gradient requires no intricate art skills, and brings the fall foliage to your fingtertips.

“Cat-eye effects give those rich colors movement and dimension, so the manicure becomes interesting through the finish itself,” says Estrada. “The color and light are doing the work, and make a statement without adding any other nail art.” All it takes is your favorite shifting polish and a magnet to get the look.

Polka dots are yes, chic, but more importantly super easy to DIY if you still want to lean into a low-effort design. Choose your favorite contrasting autumnal hues and use a dotting tool (or even a toothpick) in the lighter polish to create the look. Or go for the reverse finish, by using the lighter color as the base.

Burgundy French tips are another favorite minimalist option of Swan’s, and adding a cat-eye effect gives some dimension to the classic yet seasonal manicure.

“Deep burgundy and wine red are also beautiful seasonal choices,” Swan says. “These colors complement fall wardrobes perfectly and create that cozy autumn feeling.” Some of our favorite quintessential fall reds include essie’s Bordeaux (how fitting!) and Sally Hansen’s Pomp & Plum.

“During this time of year, warm, rich shades are especially popular, including chocolate brown, espresso, and caramel,” says Swan. To get the coffee-esque hue, try OPI’s Espresso Your Inner Self.

If you want to lean into the theme of the feast, go for this subtle pumpkin pie-coded manicure. Choose two shades of orange and create a light-to dark gradient by blending the hues outwards with a small sponge.

These translucent jelly nails have the same decadent finish as a sweet Thanksgiving treat. To get the look, apply a sheer coat or two of your favorite polish, then seal it all in with a high-shine top coat for that signature glassy effect.

Pumpkin spice season goes well into November, and this reflective manicure is proof. Start with an orange base, then layer on a silver or orange chrome topper for a metallic finish that feels festive but still wearable well beyond the big feast.

Jasmine Hyman is the assistant beauty editor at Cosmo who covers all things nails—from the latest design trends to new polish launches. For this story, she consulted celebrity nail artists for their favorite minimalist Thanksgiving nail ideas.

You’ll also likely find her in bed reading a good book or endlessly scrolling TikTok (spoiler: it’s usually the latter) while listening to Harry Styles’ entire discography on repeat. Follow her on Insta to be inundated with pictures of her meals.

#6904. 60 Best Gifts for Men That Aren't a Total Afterthought

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

Shopping for men doesn’t have to be difficult. Even when he says he doesn’t want anything or shrugs and tells you to “surprise him,” we know (because this is what we do) that there are tons of thoughtful and really cool options out there, waiting to be discovered.

If you’re already shopping for the holidays, stop wondering what to get him and look at this list already. Our gift guide for men spans everyone on your list, from the teen boy who’s into fitness and skincare to the hardworking dad who never treats himself to the polished guy who seems to already own everything worth owning.

While a gift card technically counts as a present, we think our picks are more memorable. Keep scrolling for unique, practical, and elevated ways to treat him, no matter the occasion.

Make sure he has at least one high-quality hoodie hanging in his closet. Lululemon’s Steady State SuperLoft pullover is roomy, sturdy, and made of extra-soft fleece. Choose from 11 colors.

#6900. This Charming 1960’s Alabama Cottage Unites Classic Southern Style With Family Heirlooms

There’s room for everyone, grandchildren included.

The best houses keep family close, whether that means an expansive living room that fits all the siblings and their spouses, room for another generation under the same roof, or grandparents just a few minutes down the road. For Leigh and Kevin Misso, founders of River Brook Design & Construction, that sentiment became the starting point for a deeply personal project: transforming a dated 1960s house in Mountain Brook, Alabama, into a new home for Leigh's mother and stepfather, who moved to be closer to their grandchildren.

At 2,600 square feet, the three-bedroom house wasn’t particularly grand, but that was precisely its appeal. “We wanted to embrace its modest scale rather than fight against it,” says Leigh. The couple undertook a complete renovation, adding square footage, vaulting ceilings, and reimagining the once-cramped kitchen as an airy gathering place. Throughout, the goal was to preserve the home’s cottage-like charm while giving it the breathing room to accommodate a growing family. “Every detail was thoughtfully considered to make this a place where children and grandchildren feel at home, generations can gather, and meaningful family traditions and memories can be made for years to come,” adds Leigh.

The resulting interiors are crisp and refined, while still holding decades of family history. Leigh’s grandmother's antique sideboard anchors the dining room, while her wicker sofa finds new life on the sunroom porch, dressed in blue-and-white Les Touches fabric. A four-poster bed passed down through her stepfather’s family takes pride of place in the primary bedroom. Elsewhere, mustard velvet, richly colored built-ins, and playful painted stripes add a fresh perspective to traditional Southern decorating.

Of course, the house’s greatest luxury might be its expansive rear porch, where a custom hanging daybed beckons, and where Leigh’s mother spends much of her time. It was one of the renovation’s biggest investments, but for a family whose entire project was predicated on spending more time together, the payoff was worth it. “Ultimately, our goal was to create a home that feels just as beautiful and comfortable for quiet everyday moments as it does when the entire family comes together,” says Leah.

FAST FACTSDesigner: River Brook Design & ConstructionLocation: Mountain Brook, AlabamaThe Space: A three-bedroom, two-and-a-half-bathroom home across 2,600 square feet.

“This is one of my favorite little rooms in the house,” says Leigh of the family room, which doubles as a play space. “We wanted to create a cozy, intimate retreat that felt full of personality, warmth, and charm. I love that we embraced the smaller scale of the space rather than trying to make it feel larger. The result is a room that feels incredibly inviting, layered, and collected.”

Vaulted ceilings and an exposed wood beam give this modestly sized living room an impressive sense of scale. Below, Leigh and Kevin balanced a tailored Coley Home sofa with dark upholstered seating, antique armchairs, and a Louis Philippe mirror.

“Ultimately, we wanted this room to embody the charm of the entire home—small in scale but never small in feeling,” says Leigh. “It’s a space that feels elegant enough for entertaining yet relaxed enough for grandchildren to curl up on the sofa. That balance of beauty, comfort, and livability is always at the heart of our designs.”

Proof that the smallest corners can have the biggest personalities, this charming game nook was designed with the grandchildren in mind. “I especially love the juxtaposition of the modern white pedestal table with the rich mustard velvet chairs,” adds Leigh. “The sculptural shapes and playful color bring a fresh energy to the space, while the gallery wall of collected artwork adds history, personality, and a sense of nostalgia.”

The sunroom acts a bit like a family scrapbook, compiling a slew of heirloom and inherited pieces into a comfortable place to unwind. “We wanted this space to feel as though it had been collected over generations rather than decorated all at once. It's comfortable, timeless, and a little whimsical—the perfect place for grandchildren to gather, family to visit, and everyday memories to be made.”

“I love how this dining room feels intimate, layered, and full of personality. We wanted to create a space that felt a little more formal and collected, while still being warm and inviting for everyday family gatherings,” says Leigh of the gathering space, which includes an antique sideboard that used to belong to her grandmother. “The mix of family heirlooms, traditional patterns, and unexpected pops of color gives the room a sense of history without feeling overly serious.”

A far cry from the home’s original 1960s kitchen, this reimagined space pairs vaulted ceilings with traditional, furniture-inspired details. A built-in china cabinet lends a sense of old-world formality, while marble countertops, warm wood floors, and oversized pendants keep the room feeling polished. Behind the scenes, a concealed coffee bar and appliance cabinet ensure everyday necessities don't interrupt the view.

Sleepovers get their own stylish headquarters in the grandchildren's bunk room, where the team channeled a playful and imaginative attitude. “One of my favorite details is the painted stripe treatment that wraps the walls and ceiling,” says Leigh. “Rather than using wallpaper, we chose to paint the stripes, creating a custom look that adds depth, dimension, and a wonderful sense of enclosure.”

The heart of the primary bedroom is an eye-catching antique four-poster bed, which has been pass down through several generations of Leigh’s family. “It’s such a special piece, and I love that it brings a genuine sense of history and heritage to the space,” she adds. “Rather than designing the room around something new, we embraced this treasured family heirloom and allowed it to become the focal point.”

Leigh and Kevin Misso are the husband-and-wife founders of River Brook, a Birmingham, Alabama–based design-build studio specializing in thoughtfully crafted homes that balance timeless architecture with modern livability. Together, the duo brings a refined yet approachable perspective to residential design, emphasizing traditional details, layered interiors, and spaces that feel collected rather than decorated.

#6899. What Does Transgender Mean? Everything You Need to Know, According to Experts

From gender identity to transition, here's what the term actually means.

The word "transgender" gets used a lot these days, but that doesn't necessarily indicate that everyone understands what being transgender actually means. Between headlines, social media debates, and ongoing conversations about gender identity, it's easy to feel like you're missing a piece of the puzzle or even worry about using the wrong terminology altogether. Fortunately, understanding what it means to be transgender doesn't have to be as complicated as the internet sometimes makes it seem.

"Being transgender means your gender identity doesn't align with the one you were assigned at birth. That's the whole heart of it," says licensed marriage and family therapist Adrienne Michelle, LMFT. "It doesn't tell you anything about how someone looks, what they've explored medically, or how far along they are in any journey. It's simply about your authentic sense of self being different from what you were told you were at the start."

Ultimately, gender is deeply personal, and there's no one "right" way to be transgender. Whether you're talking with a friend, supporting a loved one, or simply trying to better understand the world around you, having a basic understanding of transgender identity can make the conversation feel a whole lot less intimidating and a lot more inclusive.

The term "transgender" is an identity label that refers to people whose gender identity doesn’t match the sex they were assigned when they were born, explains therapist and LGBTQ+ educator Zoe Stoller. You'll also often hear transgender shortened to simply trans. The prefix trans- means "across" or "on the other side of," reflecting that a person's gender identity differs from the sex they were assigned at birth.

"Gender is expansive, and 'trans' is the umbrella term that often holds all of that expansiveness," says Michelle. "It isn't a single identity; it's a beautifully wide category that makes room for so many people."

The transgender umbrella includes trans men, trans women, and many nonbinary identities, though not everyone whose gender identity differs from the sex they were assigned at birth identifies as transgender. And that's completely okay. The right label is the one that feels right to the person using it.

Although they're often used interchangeably, sex and gender aren't the same thing. Sex refers to the biological characteristics someone is labeled or assigned at birth, while gender refers to a person's internal sense of who they are. You may also come across the terms AFAB/AMAB (assigned female at birth/assigned male at birth), which describe the sex someone was assigned when they were born, not their gender identity. And while babies are often labeled male or female at birth, sex itself isn't always binary, as intersex people exist too.

Once you understand that distinction, a lot of the terminology starts to make more sense. Terms like nonbinary, genderfluid, genderqueer, and gender nonconforming all describe distinct experiences or expressions of gender, but they aren't interchangeable, and they don't all automatically mean someone is transgender. (P.S. If you're looking for a deeper breakdown of those identities, check out our complete gender identity glossary.)

"Gender nonconforming is about not feeling aligned with the normative, performative ways gender is 'supposed' to show up—the expectations around how a man or woman should look, dress, or move through the world," says Michelle. "It's about expression, not identity. You can be gender nonconforming and cisgender, and you can be gender nonconforming and trans. The two sometimes overlap, but they're answering different questions: transgender is about who you are, gender nonconforming is about how you show up."

As Stoller explains, the transgender umbrella includes both binary identities (such as trans men and trans women) and many nonbinary identities, though not everyone whose gender differs from the sex they were assigned at birth identifies as transgender. Like every other aspect of identity, everyone's relationship with gender is unique.

First things first: Despite the headline right above this, there's no one way to "look" transgender.

As Stoller explains, being transgender isn't defined by surgeries, medical procedures, or any other external milestone. The only thing someone needs to "count" as transgender is feeling that the label accurately reflects who they are. For many people, that identity is deeply affirming regardless of whether they ever choose to transition.

Transitioning simply refers to the process of aligning someone's life more closely with their gender identity. "It’s important to note that transition isn't a checklist," says Michelle. "Some people explore hormones or surgery, some focus on legal or social changes, some pursue none of it, and every one of those paths is whole and valid."

Transitioning can look different for everyone, but it's generally broken into a few categories:

Some people choose to align their outward appearance with their gender identity because it's an affirming—and sometimes life-saving—way to feel seen as their authentic selves, Stoller says. Others don't transition at all or may be unable to do so for personal, financial, or safety reasons. Regardless, their gender identity is no less valid.

Now that you know what transgender means, it's worth talking about how to actually use the term. According to Michelle, "transgender" is an adjective, not a noun. "You'd say 'a transgender person' or 'transgender people,' never 'a transgender' or 'the transgenders,'" she explains. "It works just like any other descriptive word; you wouldn't call someone 'a tall,' so the same care applies here."

As Stoller explains, it's also important not to assume someone identifies as transgender unless they've shared that information with you. Even if you think someone's gender identity technically falls under the transgender umbrella, it's ultimately up to them to decide which labels feel the most accurate and affirming. When in doubt, use the language someone uses to describe themselves.

One of the most common terminology mix-ups is the difference between a trans man and a trans woman. "A trans man is someone who was assigned female at birth and now identifies and lives as a man. A trans woman was assigned male at birth and identifies and lives as a woman," explains Michelle.

It's an important distinction because these terms describe who someone is, not who they used to be. In other words, trans men are men, and trans women are women. The word trans simply provides additional context about their gender journey—it doesn't change their gender.

As Stoller notes, some trans men and trans women identify fully within the traditional gender binary, while others may also feel connected to nonbinary experiences. Like every other aspect of gender identity, there's no one "right" way to experience or describe being transgender.

The short answer? Use the pronouns someone asks you to use. It's really that simple.

As Stoller explains, people—whether they're transgender or cisgender—can use whatever pronouns feel the most affirming to them, including multiple sets of pronouns or neopronouns. Although people often associate certain pronouns with certain genders, pronouns don't always tell you someone's gender identity.

If you aren't sure which pronouns someone uses, it's okay to politely ask. And if you accidentally get it wrong, apologize, correct yourself, and move on.

Another common misconception is that being transgender somehow determines who someone is attracted to. It doesn't.

Gender identity and sexual orientation are two completely different things (and both are different from romantic orientation), Michelle explains. Gender identity describes who someone is, while sexual orientation describes who they're attracted to. That means transgender people can be straight, gay, lesbian, bisexual, pansexual, asexual, or identify with any other sexual orientation—just like cisgender people.

Like gender identity, sexuality is deeply personal. Labels can be helpful, Stoller says, but they don't always capture the full picture of someone's experience, so it's always best to use the language someone chooses for themselves.

Being a good LGBTQ ally doesn't require having all the answers. It starts with listening, staying open to learning, and treating transgender people with the same respect you'd want someone to show you.

Use the name and pronouns someone asks you to use, and don't make assumptions based on their appearance or gender identity. As Michelle explains, a trans man might use he/him pronouns, a trans woman might use she/her, and a nonbinary person might use they/them—or another set of pronouns that feels most affirming to them. If you aren't sure, it's okay to politely ask.

You may also come across the word transsexual, particularly in older books, articles, or medical literature. While some transgender people have reclaimed the term for themselves, Stoller notes that it's generally considered more outdated and medicalized than transgender, and many people find it offensive. Unless someone has specifically told you that's the label they use, it's best to avoid it.

Above all, remember that every transgender person's experience is different. Listening, respecting the language people use to describe themselves, and remaining open to learning will go a long way.

If you've made it this far because you're wondering whether the word transgender might describe you, know that you don't have to have everything figured out today. Gender identity isn't a quiz you pass or fail, and there's no deadline for understanding yourself.

As Stoller explains, there's no single way to experience being transgender because every person's relationship with gender is different. Some people transition socially, medically, or legally, while others never transition at all. None of those experiences make someone's identity any more or less real.

If you'd like support, consider talking with an LGBTQIA+-affirming therapist, connecting with trusted friends or loved ones, or exploring resources from organizations like The Trevor Project can be a good place to start. You don't have to navigate questions about your identity alone.

As Michelle puts it, "Gender is expansive, full stop." The most important thing isn't fitting neatly into someone else's definition; it's giving yourself the space to discover what feels authentic to you.

#6898. James Gunn’s DC Universe Was Weird, Ambitious, and Ultimately Unfinished

The writer-director is a casualty of Skydance’s acquisition of Warner Bros. He was building to a future that fans will now likely never see, for better or worse.

The hierarchy of power at DC Studios is changing again. On November 9, news broke that writer-director James Gunn and his producing partner Peter Safran left—later reported as ousted from—their jobs as co-CEOs and co-chairmen of DC Studios. Jeremy Latcham, a former Marvel Studios producer whose past credits include The Avengers and Gunn’s own Guardians of the Galaxy, will replace them on December 1.

Suddenly, DC fans woke up on a Monday to a new regime. But how will we look back on the four years that a prolific genre writer and director, who came up under Lloyd Kaufman’s B-movie studio Troma, oversaw an entire superhero universe?

It was a time of a lot of promise, not all of it fulfilled.

Before we start the eulogies for the DC Universe, it’s important to note that things aren’t over. Yet. Latcham will take over Gunn and Safran’s posts, so DC Studios is still up and running with more movies in the pipeline, many of which bear Gunn’s fingerprints. Man of Tomorrow, for example. The 2027 movie is a direct sequel to Superman, also written and directed by Gunn. Among its biggest moves is introducing a new Wonder Woman (rumored, but not confirmed, to be Adria Arjona). The Batman: Part II, the long-awaited sequel to Matt Reeves’s The Batman, will also open in 2028 with Gunn and Safran involved as producers. Gunn also said on Instagram they have been “engaged on another DC project which I’m writing.”

Both Gunn and Safran are expected to stay at DC, with Deadline confirming they will “continue to produce on various DC projects.” That said, Matthew Belloni at Puck published a story citing sources who say the DCU will reboot, again, after Man of Tomorrow. And at The Hollywood Reporter, Borys Kit and Aaron Crouch detailed the friction Gunn faced while at the studio, and how Gunn and Safran’s exits were forced by new Skydance leaders David Ellison and Ynon Kreiz. THR reports the DC heads felt secure after the big Warner-Paramount merger; Clayface had grossed $100 million, and Gunn was preparing a draft for the third Superman movie when they were told they were out. You can say the DCU is in a crisis right now.

For now, we can say that a critical chapter of DC is over. Gunn will no longer apply his vision and filter across all DC projects, which he must feel a little relieved about. By his own admission, which he wrote on Instagram on November 9, Gunn expressed feeling stretched thin, having to exert energy on movies and shows he wasn’t emotionally invested in to begin with. Said Gunn: “[T]he thing my ADHD hyperfocus has had the most difficulty with has been dividing my time between projects I’m incredibly passionate about and the ones I’m not so much and all the things I have to do as a studio head.”

Gunn and Safran got their jobs in late 2022, but it wasn’t until 2024 that people saw the first glimpse of their reign. Though Gunn had already worked for DC on 2021’s The Suicide Squad and created the spin-off Peacemaker, those projects were separate from other things at DC, then run by Walter Hamada. At the time, its biggest bet was Black Adam, Dwayne Johnson’s long-in-development antihero project that fell flat and led to nowhere. When Gunn took the reins of DC soon after, the former Marvel director had carte blanche to tailor the sandbox to his vision.

In January 2023, Gunn revealed his plans for DC Studios in something he called “Chapter 1: Gods and Monsters.” In a six-minute YouTube video, Gunn ran down his plans for the movies and shows that made up his master plan for the next decade of DC Studios. But for every mention of this movie or that HBO Max show, the first project carried significant weight. It was the first step, the real first impression, as a showcase of what Gunn would do with a DCU bent to his will. What icons would make it to the screen? What legend would get their spotlight? What iconic storylines might be retold on the big screen? What, really, will James Gunn’s DC become?

His first order of business was... Creature Commandos.

In fairness, Creature Commandos was a great show (if you saw it). It was an adult animated series where horror and pulp-inspired superheroes teamed up as a black ops strike force. While a show like that wouldn’t be anyone’s idea of a great introduction, Gunn’s background as the writer of movies like Tromeo & Juliet and his success with Guardians of the Galaxy for Marvel at least made sense. It basically said without ambiguity that this new DCU is being molded by no one else but Gunn.

Then, Superman premiered in summer 2025. Initially written by Gunn, the DC Studios head eventually named himself the director of DC’s first theatrical release under his stewardship. Despite my own misgivings about the movie, it was the reorientation a refreshed superhero universe needed. You simply can’t have a DC Universe without the Man of Steel, whose first appearance in Action Comics #1 in 1938 kicked off the modern superhero as we know it.

Superman made a solid first impression for DC at the cineplex. So there was plenty of optimism for the 2026 spin-off Supergirl starring Milly Alcock. Largely based on the acclaimed Tom King and Bilquis Evely graphic novel Supergirl: Woman of Tomorrow, the pseudo-sequel was poised to keep up DC’s newfound momentum. It didn’t. Supergirl hit a wall, much like Black Adam. No superhero universe is immune to duds, but given it was just DC’s second time at bat, a blemish on what still felt like a new, fresh-out-of-the-box DCU felt grave.

But one dud wasn’t enough to kneecap the DCU. The same summer saw the premiere of Lanterns, which fostered a faithful audience on HBO Sunday nights during its buzzy eight-episode first season. Despite mixed reviews, Clayface has grossed a profitable $100 million against a slim budget. Between the next Superman and Batman sequels, there was still plenty to look forward to. Until now.

James Gunn’s reign wasn’t impervious to bad news either. Since his January 2023 reveal, many of the projects have wound up on the back burner: The Authority, which had been a “passion project” for Gunn (with plenty of setup for it in Superman); Waller, a TV show with Viola Davis reprising her role from the Suicide Squad films; Paradise Lost, a political epic set in Wonder Woman’s home; and Booster Gold, a superhero comedy about a goofball from the future. And throughout his reign, Gunn put up with questions about his Batman (not Matt Reeves’s), which he would have introduced in The Brave and the Bold. Gunn sweated over the character so much that a cameo of Batman in Creature Commandos was shrouded in shadow, because he said he was still deciding on what the costume was going to look like.

Since the start of Gunn’s DC, it was clear his architecture came from a place of affection for the brand, and for very specific corners of it. His body of work reflected his scraggly upbringing as a punk creative, with empathy for characters who were too weird for their own Saturday morning cartoon. The Suicide Squad elevated such figures, like Weasel and Polka-Dot Man, whose climax involved a fight with Starro, a colossal evil starfish. Though his Superman was a sunny throwback to the Christopher Reeve classics, it was also jam-packed with the likes of Green Lantern, Mister Terrific, Metamorpho, and a villain Gunn was clearly setting up to be Bizarro.

Multiple times on social media, Gunn shared artwork from one DC book in particular: Kingdom Come, written by Mark Waid and illustrated by Alex Ross. Published in 1996, the story was a treatise of sorts by Waid, who lamented the state of superhero comics at the time. The story sees an older Superman who comes out of retirement to reform the Justice League, only to meet physical and philosophical resistance—predominantly from Batman.

Gunn’s allusions to Kingdom Come, including the exact shape of Superman’s “S” in his film, suggested he was laying the groundwork for a Kingdom Come-style event movie. It would have been an ambitious endeavor, oddly fitting to Gunn’s sensibilities. In the few years we saw Gunn at work for DC, he was building a universe where tried-and-true icons of superhero comics brushed up against the prickly and the bizarre. Where weirdness leaked from the orifices of a summer tentpole with an action figure set.

It often feels like Gunn was just getting started, but four years with two (soon three) movies written and directed by him, several TV shows—and Supergirl and Clayface, whatever you think of them—leave a big enough impression to suggest that Gunn’s DC had no shortage of imagination, even if his signature flourishes had grown familiar. Not every superhero film needs to have shock F-bombs and action scenes set to ironic licensed music, but Gunn had a lane. He excelled at staying in it.

It’s doubtful Gunn and Safran (who took on the business end of things) will stay on at DC under Skydance leadership for long. They will probably see Man of Tomorrow and The Batman: Part II through. If those movies do well, they might stick around to keep making movies, because for the most part, they have a magic that works. If the new movies fail to take off, then Gunn might finally find new stories to tell. It’s hard to say which path is the better one.

Gunn’s Kingdom Come, whatever it might have been, pointed to a future that never became a promise. In his farewell message, Gunn wrote that “the characters we set up will live on.” Whether the next era will let the DCU stay weird is another matter.

#6895. What’s Really Going On With Blake and Erika From ‘Love Is Blind’? Let’s Investigate

A new report suggests they’ve “missed each other’s company” since the show.

Confused by Blake Carver and Erika Ellis’ relationship status after Love Is Blind? You’re not alone. Though the pair called off their engagement due to “lifestyle differences” and claim they’re nothing more than friends, fans of the Netflix reality show keep raising eyebrows at their recent sightings.

“I would say, our relationship — even though it didn’t end in the traditional way that the show was supposed to end — we fell in love with each other. I’ll never not love Erika. She’ll always have a piece of my heart,” he explained.

On top of ^^^ all that, Erika is out here confusing everyone by reposting UsWeekly’s report of Blake’s quotes on her Instagram Stories.

#6894. The 12 Best Amber Colognes to Buy Right Now

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

These are the season’s top cold-weather compliment getters.

It has been an unusually warm fall so far, but today, at least in New York City, there’s finally a chill in the air. I got so excited when I saw the temperature was in the low 50s this morning that I may have rushed the season a tad and put on a thick sweater under an insulated jacket. So, I was sweating in the subway, but at least I smelled good—because when it comes to cold-weather fragrance, I know what I’m doing.

If you’re looking to add to your winter cologne rotation, there’s no better place to start than a good amber scent. When you think of fragrance as an accessory, it becomes something you strive to match with your outfit, and amber colognes pair perfectly with corduroy, thick coats, and soft scarves. But what exactly does amber smell like? After all, the kind of amber that probably comes to mind—fossilized tree amber—doesn’t actually have much of a scent.

Well, when we say “amber” in the fragrance world, it really refers to a blend of natural resins and synthetics that combine to produce a warm, rich, and sweet smell. Some classic scent notes that make up the amber fragrance family include labdanum (a resinous extract from a Mediterranean shrub), benzoin (a sweet, balsamic tree resin), ambroxan (a synthetic fragrance molecule with a warm, woody aroma), vanilla, and tonka bean.

Here, we’ve pulled together our definitive list of the top amber scents for men, covering every vibe, lifestyle, and scent story you’re trying to tell. Whether you’re leaning into smoky sophistication, something on the sweeter side, or going for “I naturally smell this good,” there’s something here for everyone. And with the holidays fast approaching, consider these foolproof gift options for anyone on your list who appreciates smelling like they have their life together.

Inspired by the luxury fashion label’s most iconic men’s silhouette, this fragrance is designed to capture the essence of a tuxedo, using notes that convey sophistication and sharp black-tie tailoring into scent. Grounded in earthy patchouli and layered with subtle spice, it radiates a refined, confident energy that’s perfect for winter yet versatile enough for wearing it year-round.

The purpose of Maison Margiela’s Replica line is that the scents are made to capture memories and moods, the scent of a specific moment. By the Fireplace is easily one of the most evocative scents in the collection—my personal favorite, for what it’s worth—because smoke and warm chestnut instantly transport you somewhere near a warm crackling fire.

It starts with a bit of spice and smoke, and once it settles on the skin, that initial smokiness softens into a smooth, vanilla warmth. The rest of the fragrance leans very heavily into the sweetness of amber. It’s a compliment-getter, if that’s what you’re after.

If you want to channel your inner Alexander Skarsgård in Pillion (IYKYK) with a scent that radiates rugged confidence, subtle earthiness, and that intoxicating new-leather smell that only comes from a new pair of boots or a leather trench, look no further. Malin + Goetz’s Leather Eau de Parfum has been a staple in my collection for years

It’s leathery and musky, but it has a good hit of cedar that combines to leans it into the world of amber. It also has a lot of floral notes as it dries on the skin to balance it all out. It’s a nicely layered fragrance. A purist might not call this amber, but we think it’s well within the sweet, resinous world of these other fragrances.

Not ready to commit to the full bottle? The unisex cologne also comes as a perfume oil in a compact rollerball perfect for a trial run or as a stocking stuffer.

As I noted in my intro, amber fragrances can take on a variety of scent profiles, all capturing the warmth and depth of true amber. Rich gourmand options like Horace’s Vintage Vanilla are a great pick if you love scents inspired by the culinary world, but don’t worry, it’s expertly balanced with a mix of woodiness and spice so you won’t end up smelling like you went overboard on your marshmallow-y Victoria’s Secret body mist.

Don’t even want to smell like an edible vanilla confection? No problem. The brand’s signature Oud Rose is another solid amber-forward scent that leans on floral notes to balance its intensity.

Diptyque is another one of those bougie fragrance houses everyone recognizes, yet for some reason I rarely reach for their scents over my tried-and-true favorites. But after smelling L’Eau Trois Eau de Toilette, I might have to rethink that stance. This one immediately transported me to being in Italy and stepping into a Duomo, where incense and myrrh linger in the air.

It takes a surprisingly verdant and green approach to the amber family, but those resinous top notes anchor everything with a spicy-smoky warmth. The result is a scent that feels refined, atmospheric, and just mysterious enough to keep you coming back for another sniff.

Sure, amber scents can be sweet and gourmand, but they can also be spicy and sophisticated. Giardini di Toscana’s Bianco Oro is a great example of the latter. At first spritz, you mostly get spicy pink and black pepper with a backdrop of amber, but as the scent lingers, the rich amber notes take center stage along with smooth musks and creamy woods. As it fades, it becomes a bit powdery, but the trio of spices, amber, and musks are still there. It’s the perfect scent to wear out to a nice dinner or evening event, but it works just as well in the daytime.

It’s a general rule that amber and woods go great together, since amber provides a warm richness that blends well with dry, crisp woods. That combination is at the foundation of Dolce & Gabbana’s The One for Men EDP, but before we get there, we actually get a touch of bright and juicy citrus from Tarocco orange and grapefruit as well as a natural, herbal hint of clary sage at the heart that adds a bit of earthiness to this fragrance. Amber and woods mingle at the base, grounding the lighter, more vibrant opening in something warm and rich.

Guerlain makes a handful of luxury amber fragrances, but Tobacco Honey is the one that stands out from the rest. It’s an intense blend of—you guessed it—tobacco leaves and golden honey, which creates a beautifully bold contrast of smoky and sweet. It emphasizes the sweet side of the ambery tobacco accord at the heart of this scent, enveloping the dry tobacco notes in vanilla, tonka bean, and sesame to create a rich, creamy heart that gets lifted up by vibrant, sweet honey. If you’ve ever wanted to try a tobacco fragrance but worried it would smell too smoky (or too much like cigarettes), this is the one that will change your mind.

For something a bit lighter and more airy than the mostly rich and deep amber scents on this list, try Golden Morning from Lafco’s new (and first-ever) personal fragrance collection. Like a cup of hot chamomile tea with honey, it’s soothing, warm, and a little sweet. Bright and citrusy at the top with bergamot and apple blossom, it then introduces chamomile and a floral heart of lavender, violet leaf, and rose. The dry down is smooth and sweet with honey, tonka bean, amber, and musks.

Okay, I’m not saying the other fragrances on this list can’t also be considered “bougie,” but Maison Francis Kurkdjian Paris’ Baccarat Rouge 540 takes luxury to an entirely new level. And yes, while the price tag is particularly eye-watering, I have to admit: this scent is absolutely worth it. A perfectly balanced blend of woody, floral, and ambery notes, this fragrance uses high-quality ingredients with a higher molecular weight, meaning they cling to the skin longer and create a richer scent profile with impressive staying power. In short, a little goes a very long way with this one…if that helps you justify the price tag.

Sitting somewhere between gourmand and citrus on the fragrance spectrum, Arquiste’s L’Or de Louis is one of those amber scents that just radiates richness. The 24K gold flakes are a little too on-the-nose, but the the story behind the fragrance does a better job of telling a story of opulence. It was inspired by the moments when King Louis XIV and his court strolled Versailles’ Grand Orangery, enjoying the flowering orange trees warmed by bonfires.

It also won The Fragrance Foundation’s Indie Fragrance of the Year Award in 2024, so the hoity-toity storytelling is backed by a scent that actually smells…well…good. The result is a golden aura of perfectly blended smokiness, fruitiness, and depth. A fragrance truly fit for a king.

If your nose is on the more sensitive side and you’re looking for a warm, enveloping scent that doesn’t overwhelm with heavy muskiness, D’Orsay’s Te dire oui. V.H. Eau de Parfum is a perfect choice. Built on leather, it avoids the overpowering muskiness found in many amber and leather fragrances, opting instead for a cleaner, sportier interpretation rounded out by a subtle saffron and rose finish.

This aligns perfectly with the brand’s philosophy, which favors fragrances that act as personal rituals—subtle companions that are seamlessly woven into everyday life—rather than scents that dominate every room you enter.

#6892. I Asked Designers for Their Secrets to an Expensive-Looking Guest Bedroom. They All Said the Same Thing

Skip the fussy pillow pile and focus on the details guests will actually notice.

Pictured above: a bold aubergine double guest bedroom designed by Nicole Fisher.

The holiday hosting season is upon us, and while you’re planning your tablescape decor and seasonal trimmings, you might want to take a hard look at your guest bedroom.

“Most guest rooms get treated like overflow storage, and guests feel that the second they walk in,” says Marie Cloud, principal designer at Indigo Pruitt.

Even if you host only occasionally, your guest room should give visitors a comfortable place to unwind. No need for a guest book or fuzzy slippers: Designers say these four thoughtful touches can make the room feel luxe without costing a fortune.

Regardless of the design aesthetic, the ambiance of a room always hinges on lighting. “I always pay attention to the lighting,” says Louis Lin, founder of his eponymous New York City–based interior design studio. “A beautiful bedside lamp with a warm, flattering glow can transform the atmosphere of a room. Layering different light sources, rather than relying on overhead lighting, can do that. It's a relatively simple gesture that makes a room feel much more intimate and refined.” This also gives guests the freedom to tailor the air of their space to their liking. It’s one of the few details they can truly control in their temporary lodging.

While a pile of pillows might look inviting, designer Marie Cloud says the bedtime legwork promptly cancels out the aesthetics.

“Skip the stack of eight decorative pillows your guest has to relocate before bed,” says Cloud. Her perfect formula for a top-notch guest setup? Two sleeping pillows, plus one lumbar pillow, all set atop a good set of sheets.

“Sheets with real hand-feel, like a crisp percale or washed linen,” Cloud suggests. “Finish with a throw folded at the foot in a color pulled from the room. It looks considered, and it’s actually comfortable at 2 a.m.”

Most guest rooms get the all-white wall treatment in the name of neutrality, but all it really says is that your space lacks intention. Just because this room is (mostly) reserved for guests doesn't mean it has to lack character. In fact, Cloud says you should not hold back.

“Drench the room,” says the designer. “Walls, trim, and ceiling in the same color, ideally something deep and warm. Continuous color reads custom, it makes a small room feel wrapped rather than cramped, and it tells your guest the space was designed for them. A guest room is also the lowest-risk room in the house to be bold, so take the swing.”

The best guest setups make visitors feel anticipated, even when they show up unexpectedly. To achieve the boutique-hotel feel, designers recommend investing in small luxuries that leave a big impact. A few items Cloud and Lin always recommend having on hand are a small luggage rack, real hangers, fresh towels, and a select set of toiletries reserved especially for guests.

“The real luxury is making guests feel that the room has been prepared just for them,” says Lin.

Follow House Beautiful on Instagram and TikTok.

#6891. Time to Revisit Julianne Hough’s Dating History for Some Seriously Hunky Reasons

The concept of her attending the Met Gala with Ryan Seacrest… twice.

Julianne Hough has graced our screens for nearly two decades thanks to her ties to Dancing With the Stars and projects like Safe Haven and Burlesque. For someone who’s been in the entertainment biz for a minute, Julianne has surprisingly kept her personal life under wraps. Well, for the most part.

Aside from her music, dancing skills, and red carpet looks, the media personality has also made headlines for her rumored romances and high-profile relationships. (Because who could forget she dated Ryan Seacrest back in the day? Certainly not this writer who lived through it.)

Anyway! As she interviews celebs in the DWTS sky box and joins them for numbers on the dance floor, you might be wondering who she’s romantically linked to these days. Ahead, find Julianne Hough’s full dating history, including some surprise rumors.

Before Julianne moved out to L.A. to be a pro on DWTS’s early seasons, she was romantically linked to her childhood sweetheart, Zack Wilson, who she had known since she was nine.

“I was engaged to someone else, a guy I knew from Utah who had served a Mormon mission,” she said during a 2012 interview with Glamour, adding that he was “a great guy.”

They ended up calling off their engagement in November 2007, and according to Julianne, it fizzled out because she was ~soul-searching.~

“I needed to figure myself out more, and so I moved to L.A. and we ended it,” she shared, adding that he went on to marry and have a kid with her childhood best friend.

“It was a little weird when it happened, but they’re a great couple!” she admitted.

Okay, so her engagement didn’t work out—but it led to her first high-profile relationship with country singer Chuck Wicks after they met on Brad Paisley’s tour in August 2008.

It was truly a whirlwind romance, since he publicly declared they were “very much in love” on red carpets and even gave her a birthday shoutout on stage. By December that year, they had apparently discussed potentially getting married.

Of course, the pair walked many a red carpet and even became partners on DWTS in 2009.

They had reportedly had a “mutual” split by November 2009, and though things seemed amicable at the time, Chuck admitted that “shit hit the fan” nearly a decade later.

“I’m not going to throw anybody under the bus but … it wasn’t my fault,” he said on his Talk to Chuck podcast in 2020, adding that he “played the game” and kept things civil in public, though he “didn’t really like her at the time.”

“Now, I just don't even care,” he admitted. “It’s on you. You get past it. I’ve seen her one time. I’ve seen her one time in 10 years.”

There’s not much to go on here, since the Safe Haven actor and comedian never confirmed whether they were an item. However, in March 2010, a source told People that Dane had “kissed her, and they quickly left together” after he headlined a comedy show.

They were seen together in West Hollywood one more time before moving on.

Though Julianne claimed a friend tried to set her up with the American Idol host when she was still engaged to Zack, she didn’t give him a chance until things ended with Chuck years later. During the aforementioned Glamour interview, she revealed she had properly met Ryan when she and Chuck appeared on his radio show as a couple.

“I blush when I hear [the interview] because Ryan is hitting on me the entire time!” she shared. “At the end, Ryan said something like, ‘You guys really have something special!’ And I thought to myself, ‘He’s so full of shit right now!’ When Chuck and I broke up, I wanted to be single. That lasted five months. Then Ryan and I went on a date.”

Her interview with Chuck was seemingly scrubbed from the internet (womp), but she appeared on Ryan’s radio show many times after. This was pretty much the vibe every time:

Anyway, their one date eventually turned into a 3-year relationship, complete with Golden Globes, Emmys, and two Met Gala appearances.

According to People’s sources, they eventually split in March 2013 because of “busy work schedules,” though they’re still cool to this day.

When asked whether it was awkward for the exes to chat on-record during a joint interview with her brother, Derek Hough, in 2022, Ryan said, “There is nothing uncomfortable about it because we remained friends for years and still are.”

Julianne maintained that they still had “respect and love” for each other after their split.

After her split with Ryan, Julianne was reportedly linked to Oscar-winning filmmaker Ari Sandel in July 2013 when they were spotted making out at Nobu.

In August 2013, they were photographed leaving a party at the Chateau Marmont and grabbed brunch in West Hollywood the next day. They never publicly commented on their relationship, so it’s unclear how long they actually dated, if at all.

This is Julianne’s biggest and most public relationship to date. She met former hockey star Brooks Laich through mutual friends before they started a long-distance relationship in 2013. (He lived in Washington, D.C. while Julianne was still based in L.A.)

They eventually got engaged in 2015 and tied the knot at an outdoor ceremony on Idaho’s Lake Coeur d’Alene in 2017. However, their romance would come to an end by May 2020, when they announced their split in a joint statement to People.

“We have lovingly and carefully taken the time we have needed to arrive at our decision to separate. We share an abundance of love and respect for one another and will continue to lead with our hearts from that place,” they said at the time.

During an August 2024 appearance on Dax Shepard’s Armchair Expert podcast, Julianne admitted that she and Brooks were “sad that it didn’t work out” between them—especially since he’s remained close with her brother, Derek.

“I feel like our relationship and our marriage was exactly right, and the end of it is right, too,” she explained at the time. “We both have regrets that it didn’t work out because I don’t think we had the maturity to come together. He was contracting while I was expanding. We just couldn’t find each other in that.”

Back in December 2024, Julianne was photographed after a dinner date with Bachelor Nation alum Tanner Courtad. That was the only time we had seen anything about the pair…until Tanner fully spilled the tea during the Bachelor Happy Hour podcast in November 2026.

“That was short-lived, but it was fun. I was going to Dancing With the Stars to support Joey [Graziadei], and I was sitting in the front row, and Ezra [Sosa] came over to me, and he goes, ‘Hey, our host thinks you’re cute. Can I give you her number?’” he shared.

He continued, “I actually met her out in the parking lot. We talked for a couple of minutes, decided to go on a date and talked for a little bit. We went to the park with her dog, got some sushi, hung out for the night and that’s when we got spotted by the paparazzi is picking up sushi.”

Though they never kept seeing each other, Tanner confirmed, “It was very short-lived. Julianne’s great, but it was never anything more than just, like, a singular date.”

That’s all for now, but we’re actively rooting for Julianne and her hot girl agenda.
